### PHASE 10: Agents, stateful workflows and interoperable tools — Days 271–300
#### Module 55: Agent vs tool call vs ordinary workflow — Days 271–275 (Intermediate)
- **Goal / deliverable:** Build the smallest agent execution loop
- **Why it matters:** An agent combines model decisions, tools and state in a bounded execution loop.
- **Simple .NET comparison:** A workflow is your fixed C# pipeline; an agent gets to choose some next steps.
- **Example:** Support agent reads an order, checks policy and drafts next action.
- **What to build:** Build the smallest agent execution loop
- **Concepts introduced:** Agent loop, State, Tool budget, Termination, Workflow
- **Supports practical project:** `p11` — Advanced Enterprise RAG
- **Common mistake to avoid:** Trying to memorize terms instead of running a tiny .NET experiment.
- **Official / external references:** [Microsoft Learn — Agent Framework course](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/agent-framework/get-started/) (`agentstart`); [Microsoft Agent Framework for .NET](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet) (`agent`); [Agent Framework — numbered .NET samples](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet/samples/01-get-started) (`agentsamples`)

**Existing C# starter illustration — Bounded agent loop — pseudocode:**
```csharp
for (int step = 0; step < maxSteps; step++) {
    var proposal = await AskModelAsync(state);
    if (proposal.IsFinal) return proposal.Answer;
    ValidateToolArguments(proposal);
    AuthorizeUserAndTool(proposal);
    if (proposal.IsSensitive) await RequireHumanApproval();
    await ExecuteToolWithTimeoutAsync(proposal);
}
throw new Exception("Agent step limit reached");
// Names are illustrative, not drop-in framework API.
```
**Day-by-day tasks, explanations and proof:**

**Day 271: Define the difference** — *Understand*
- **Understand:** A single tool call is not necessarily a long-lived autonomous agent.
- **Do:** Compare 3 diagrams: LLM answer, tool call and 3-step agent loop.
- **Definition of done / proof:** Three system patterns
- **Read next:** [Microsoft Learn — Agent Framework course](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/agent-framework/get-started/) (`agentstart`); [Microsoft Agent Framework for .NET](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet) (`agent`); [Agent Framework — numbered .NET samples](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet/samples/01-get-started) (`agentsamples`)

**Day 272: Instructions, tools and state** — *Try a tiny example*
- **Understand:** An agent needs a goal, allowed tools and state.
- **Do:** Create AgentState with RequestId, Messages, ToolCount and MaxSteps.
- **Definition of done / proof:** Typed agent state
- **Read next:** [Microsoft Learn — Agent Framework course](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/agent-framework/get-started/) (`agentstart`); [Microsoft Agent Framework for .NET](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet) (`agent`); [Agent Framework — numbered .NET samples](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet/samples/01-get-started) (`agentsamples`)

**Day 273: Execute one loop** — *Write C# / build*
- **Understand:** Ask model for a safe tool or final answer, then continue with results.
- **Do:** Implement pseudo-agent switch with two fake tools.
- **Definition of done / proof:** Bounded loop demonstration
- **Read next:** [Microsoft Learn — Agent Framework course](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/agent-framework/get-started/) (`agentstart`); [Microsoft Agent Framework for .NET](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet) (`agent`); [Agent Framework — numbered .NET samples](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet/samples/01-get-started) (`agentsamples`)

**Day 274: Plan for termination** — *Test your understanding*

- **Do:** Add MaxSteps=4 and timeout; simulate a looping model.
- **Definition of done / proof:** Safe stop behavior

**Day 275: Prefer simple code for fixed tasks** — *Finish and explain*

#### Module 56: Conversation, working, semantic and long-term memory — Days 276–280 (Intermediate)
- **Goal / deliverable:** Design privacy-aware agent memory
- **Why it matters:** Different kinds of memory belong in different stores; not everything is an embedding.
- **Simple .NET comparison:** SQL is for durable business facts; Redis is session state; vectors help find similar notes.
- **Example:** Customer shipping address is not a fuzzy “memory”—read it from the source database.
- **What to build:** Design privacy-aware agent memory
- **Concepts introduced:** Short-term memory, Working memory, Long-term memory, Semantic memory, Compaction
- **Supports practical project:** `p11` — Advanced Enterprise RAG
- **Common mistake to avoid:** Trying to memorize terms instead of running a tiny .NET experiment.
- **Official / external references:** [Microsoft Learn — Agent Framework course](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/agent-framework/get-started/) (`agentstart`); [Microsoft Agent Framework for .NET](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet) (`agent`); [Microsoft.Extensions.AI — Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/microsoft-extensions-ai) (`mea`)

**Existing C# starter illustration — Short-term state and long-term memory differ:**
```csharp
public record ConversationMessage(string Role, string Text);
public record SessionState(string SessionId, List<ConversationMessage> History);
// Do not treat old chat turns as an authorized source of business facts.
```

#### Module 58: Human approvals, durable execution and workflow design — Days 286–290 (Intermediate)
- **Goal / deliverable:** Build controlled support workflows
- **Why it matters:** Sensitive actions need explicit approval, and long jobs need persisted checkpoints.
- **Simple .NET comparison:** Approval is a business workflow state, not a polite line in a prompt.
- **Example:** Refund tool returns PendingApproval until an authorized human confirms it.
- **What to build:** Build controlled support workflows
- **Concepts introduced:** Human-in-the-loop, Checkpoint, Durable workflow, Approval, Idempotency
- **Supports practical project:** `p12` — Customer Support Agent
- **Common mistake to avoid:** Trying to memorize terms instead of running a tiny .NET experiment.
- **Official / external references:** [Agent Framework — .NET workflows](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet/samples/03-workflows) (`agentworkflow`); [Microsoft Learn — Agent Framework course](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/agent-framework/get-started/) (`agentstart`); [Microsoft Entra identity platform](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity-platform/) (`identity`)

**Existing C# starter illustration — Write operations have an explicit approval state:**
```csharp
enum ToolDecision { Allowed, NeedsApproval, Denied }
ToolDecision decision = ToolDecision.NeedsApproval;
if (decision != ToolDecision.Allowed)
    Console.WriteLine("No mutation is executed yet.");
```
**Day-by-day tasks, explanations and proof:**

**Day 286: Read vs write permissions** — *Understand*
- **Understand:** Read-only tools are lower risk than refunds or cancellations.
- **Do:** Classify all your tools as read, write or sensitive.
- **Definition of done / proof:** Tool permission matrix
- **Read next:** [Agent Framework — .NET workflows](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet/samples/03-workflows) (`agentworkflow`); [Microsoft Learn — Agent Framework course](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/agent-framework/get-started/) (`agentstart`); [Microsoft Entra identity platform](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity-platform/) (`identity`)

- **Definition of done / proof:** Approval workflow
- **Read next:** [Agent Framework — .NET workflows](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet/samples/03-workflows) (`agentworkflow`); [Microsoft Learn — Agent Framework course](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/agent-framework/get-started/) (`agentstart`); [Microsoft Entra identity platform](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity-platform/) (`identity`)

- **Read next:** [Agent Framework — .NET workflows](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet/samples/03-workflows) (`agentworkflow`); [Microsoft Learn — Agent Framework course](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/agent-framework/get-started/) (`agentstart`); [Microsoft Entra identity platform](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity-platform/) (`identity`)

**Day 289: Make side effects idempotent** — *Test your understanding*

- **Definition of done / proof:** Retry-safe ticket write

#### Module 59: Sequential, parallel and reviewer agent workflows — Days 291–295 (Intermediate)
- **Goal / deliverable:** Build the Researcher + Reviewer workflow
- **Why it matters:** Multiple agents are useful only when they add measurable quality or separation.
- **Simple .NET comparison:** C# Task.WhenAll is parallel work; the reviewer step is an explicit quality gate.
- **Example:** Researcher → Reviewer → Writer is clearer than 10 agents chatting endlessly.
- **What to build:** Build the Researcher + Reviewer workflow
- **Concepts introduced:** Sequential workflow, Parallel orchestration, Reviewer agent, Handoff, Termination rule
- **Supports practical project:** `p13` — Researcher + Reviewer Workflow
- **Common mistake to avoid:** Trying to memorize terms instead of running a tiny .NET experiment.
- **Official / external references:** [Agent Framework — .NET workflows](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet/samples/03-workflows) (`agentworkflow`); [Agent Framework — numbered .NET samples](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet/samples/01-get-started) (`agentsamples`); [Microsoft Agent Framework for .NET](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet) (`agent`)

**Existing C# starter illustration — Workflow choices — plain diagram:**
```csharp
Sequential: Research -> Review -> Edit
Parallel:   ResearchA + ResearchB -> Merge
Approval:   ProposedRefund -> WaitForHuman -> Execute
Rule: Prefer a simple C# function when the steps are known.
```
**Day-by-day tasks, explanations and proof:**

**Day 291: Sequential workflow** — *Understand*
- **Understand:** Output from one step becomes input for the next.
- **Do:** Build Researcher → Reviewer pipeline with two framework agents.
- **Definition of done / proof:** Sequential research draft
- **Read next:** [Agent Framework — .NET workflows](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet/samples/03-workflows) (`agentworkflow`); [Agent Framework — numbered .NET samples](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet/samples/01-get-started) (`agentsamples`); [Microsoft Agent Framework for .NET](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet) (`agent`)

**Day 292: Parallel research** — *Try a tiny example*
- **Understand:** Independent tasks may run concurrently with controlled aggregation.

#### Module 60: MCP server, client and agent-to-agent basics — Days 296–300 (Intermediate)
- **Goal / deliverable:** Build a C# MCP Server and Client
- **Why it matters:** MCP standardizes how AI clients discover and call external tools, prompts and resources.
- **Simple .NET comparison:** MCP is an adapter protocol; your authorization and business services remain ordinary .NET.
- **Example:** An MCP tool get_order uses the same IOrderService as your ASP.NET API.
- **What to build:** Build a C# MCP Server and Client
- **Concepts introduced:** MCP, MCP server, MCP client, Tools/resources/prompts, A2A
- **Supports practical project:** `p14` — C# MCP Server and Client
- **Common mistake to avoid:** Trying to memorize terms instead of running a tiny .NET experiment.
- **Official / external references:** [Microsoft Learn — Get started with .NET MCP](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/get-started-mcp) (`mcplearn`); [Official MCP C# SDK](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/csharp-sdk) (`mcpgit`); [MCP specification and concepts](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/docs/getting-started/intro) (`mcpprotocol`); [A2A protocol documentation](https://a2a-protocol.org/latest/) (`agent2agent`)

**Existing C# starter illustration — MCP relationship — architecture:**
```csharp
ASP.NET / AI Host
    -> MCP Client (C#)
        -> MCP Server (C#)
            -> get_order_status TOOL
                -> IOrderService / SQL

Tools are discoverable. Caller authorization is still mandatory.
```
**Day-by-day tasks, explanations and proof:**

**Day 296: Understand host/client/server** — *Understand*
- **Understand:** An AI host uses an MCP client to talk to an MCP server.
- **Do:** Draw AI app → MCP client → MCP server → IOrderService.
- **Definition of done / proof:** MCP architecture
- **Read next:** [Microsoft Learn — Get started with .NET MCP](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/get-started-mcp) (`mcplearn`); [Official MCP C# SDK](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/csharp-sdk) (`mcpgit`); [MCP specification and concepts](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/docs/getting-started/intro) (`mcpprotocol`)

**Day 297: Tools, resources and prompts** — *Try a tiny example*
- **Understand:** Tools perform operations; resources expose data; prompts are reusable patterns.
- **Do:** List two tools, one read-only resource and one prompt for order support.
- **Definition of done / proof:** MCP capability inventory
- **Read next:** [Microsoft Learn — Get started with .NET MCP](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/get-started-mcp) (`mcplearn`); [Official MCP C# SDK](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/csharp-sdk) (`mcpgit`); [MCP specification and concepts](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/docs/getting-started/intro) (`mcpprotocol`)

**Day 298: Write MCP server in C#** — *Write C# / build*
- **Understand:** Use the official MCP C# SDK rather than a Python tutorial.
- **Do:** Expose get_order_status from a small .NET host with fake database.
- **Definition of done / proof:** Running C# MCP server
- **Read next:** [Microsoft Learn — Get started with .NET MCP](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/get-started-mcp) (`mcplearn`); [Official MCP C# SDK](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/csharp-sdk) (`mcpgit`); [MCP specification and concepts](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/docs/getting-started/intro) (`mcpprotocol`)

**Day 299: Connect and authorize a client** — *Test your understanding*
- **Understand:** Transport and discovery do not replace caller authentication/permissions.
- **Do:** Call server tool from a C# MCP client and reject an unauthorized request.
- **Definition of done / proof:** Client + auth demo
- **Read next:** [Microsoft Learn — Get started with .NET MCP](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/get-started-mcp) (`mcplearn`); [Official MCP C# SDK](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/csharp-sdk) (`mcpgit`); [MCP specification and concepts](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/docs/getting-started/intro) (`mcpprotocol`)

**Day 300: A2A is a different layer** — *Finish and explain*
- **Understand:** A2A relates to communication between agents, not the same as MCP tools.
- **Do:** Draw MCP vs A2A diagram; note when agent interoperability is useful.
- **Definition of done / proof:** MCP vs A2A comparison
- **Read next:** [Microsoft Learn — Get started with .NET MCP](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/get-started-mcp) (`mcplearn`); [Official MCP C# SDK](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/csharp-sdk) (`mcpgit`); [MCP specification and concepts](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/docs/getting-started/intro) (`mcpprotocol`)

**Current module-level recipe (cross-check against day records):**

- Day 297: List two tools, one read-only resource and one prompt for order support.
- Day 298: Expose get_order_status from a small .NET host with fake database.
- Day 299: Call server tool from a C# MCP client and reject an unauthorized request.
- Day 300: Draw MCP vs A2A diagram; note when agent interoperability is useful.

- Day 297: MCP capability inventory

### PHASE 11: Beyond chat: multimodal, local and model internals — Days 301–330
#### Module 61: Images, OCR and Azure Document Intelligence — Days 301–305 (Advanced)
- **Goal / deliverable:** Build the Invoice Intake Assistant
- **Why it matters:** Images may contain text, tables, layout and objects, requiring different processing choices.
- **Simple .NET comparison:** OCR extracts characters; vision interpretation describes visual content; layouts need structured extraction.
- **Example:** An invoice image needs exact totals and field coordinates, not just a casual caption.
- **What to build:** Build the Invoice Intake Assistant
- **Concepts introduced:** Computer vision, OCR, Layout extraction, Azure Document Intelligence, Confidence
- **Supports practical project:** `p15` — Invoice + Voice Mini Assistant
- **Common mistake to avoid:** Trying to memorize terms instead of running a tiny .NET experiment.
- **Official / external references:** [Azure AI Vision](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/computer-vision/) (`vision`); [Azure AI Document Intelligence](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/document-intelligence/) (`docintel`); [Azure RAG demo — ASP.NET + Blazor (C#)](https://github.com/Azure-Samples/azure-search-openai-demo-csharp) (`ragcsharp`)

**Existing C# starter illustration — OCR extracted values still need typed review:**
```csharp
public record ExtractedInvoice(
    string? InvoiceNumber, decimal? Total, bool NeedsHumanReview);
// Compare line item totals using deterministic decimal arithmetic.
```
**Day-by-day tasks, explanations and proof:**

**Day 301: Vision classification and captioning** — *Understand*
- **Understand:** Image models can label objects and describe scenes but can still make errors.
- **Do:** Compare image classification, object detection and captioning in a small decision table.
- **Definition of done / proof:** Image-task matrix
- **Read next:** [Azure AI Vision](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/computer-vision/) (`vision`); [Azure AI Document Intelligence](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/document-intelligence/) (`docintel`); [Azure RAG demo — ASP.NET + Blazor (C#)](https://github.com/Azure-Samples/azure-search-openai-demo-csharp) (`ragcsharp`)

**Day 302: OCR means reading characters** — *Try a tiny example*
- **Understand:** OCR extracts text, particularly from scans and photos.
- **Do:** Run one sample receipt through Document Intelligence or review the official .NET sample.
- **Definition of done / proof:** Extracted text and confidence
- **Read next:** [Azure AI Vision](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/computer-vision/) (`vision`); [Azure AI Document Intelligence](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/document-intelligence/) (`docintel`); [Azure RAG demo — ASP.NET + Blazor (C#)](https://github.com/Azure-Samples/azure-search-openai-demo-csharp) (`ragcsharp`)

**Day 303: Structured document extraction** — *Write C# / build*
- **Understand:** Invoices need line items, totals and key/value fields.
- **Do:** Map extracted fields into C# InvoiceDto with nullable values.
- **Definition of done / proof:** Typed invoice DTO
- **Read next:** [Azure AI Vision](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/computer-vision/) (`vision`); [Azure AI Document Intelligence](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/document-intelligence/) (`docintel`); [Azure RAG demo — ASP.NET + Blazor (C#)](https://github.com/Azure-Samples/azure-search-openai-demo-csharp) (`ragcsharp`)

**Day 304: Validate numbers in backend** — *Test your understanding*
- **Understand:** Never trust an image model for exact money calculations without checking.
- **Do:** Check sum of line items vs invoice total and flag mismatch.
- **Definition of done / proof:** Invoice validation result
- **Read next:** [Azure AI Vision](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/computer-vision/) (`vision`); [Azure AI Document Intelligence](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/document-intelligence/) (`docintel`); [Azure RAG demo — ASP.NET + Blazor (C#)](https://github.com/Azure-Samples/azure-search-openai-demo-csharp) (`ragcsharp`)

**Day 305: Make errors reviewable** — *Finish and explain*
- **Understand:** Low-confidence or missing fields should require human verification.
- **Do:** Design a Blazor review form highlighting doubtful fields.
- **Definition of done / proof:** Invoice review UI
- **Read next:** [Azure AI Vision](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/computer-vision/) (`vision`); [Azure AI Document Intelligence](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/document-intelligence/) (`docintel`); [Azure RAG demo — ASP.NET + Blazor (C#)](https://github.com/Azure-Samples/azure-search-openai-demo-csharp) (`ragcsharp`)

- Day 302: Run one sample receipt through Document Intelligence or review the official .NET sample.

#### Module 62: Audio, speech and real-time interaction — Days 306–310 (Advanced)
- **Goal / deliverable:** Build a small voice-note assistant
- **Why it matters:** Speech apps combine recording, speech-to-text, language processing and text-to-speech.
- **Simple .NET comparison:** It is an async streaming pipeline with latency, privacy and interruption concerns.
- **Example:** Voice complaint → transcribed text → AI ticket extraction → spoken confirmation.
- **What to build:** Build a small voice-note assistant
- **Concepts introduced:** Speech-to-text, Text-to-speech, VAD, Streaming, Audio privacy
- **Supports practical project:** `p15` — Invoice + Voice Mini Assistant
- **Common mistake to avoid:** Trying to memorize terms instead of running a tiny .NET experiment.
- **Official / external references:** [Azure Speech service](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/speech-service/) (`speech`); [Microsoft Learn — AI for .NET](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/) (`aihome`); [Generative AI for Beginners — .NET](https://github.com/microsoft/Generative-AI-for-beginners-dotnet) (`genai`)

```csharp
public record Transcript(string Text, string? Language, bool IsFinal);
// Confirm spoken business changes explicitly before executing tools.
```

**Day 310: Privacy and consent** — *Finish and explain*

#### Module 63: Multimodal apps: images, tables, audio and video — Days 311–315 (Advanced)
- **Goal / deliverable:** Extend support assistant for mixed inputs
- **Why it matters:** Multimodal systems use more than one data modality and need consistent evidence.
- **Simple .NET comparison:** A Blazor form can attach text and image together just like a multipart upload.
- **Example:** A user uploads a faulty-product photo plus text to create a support case.
- **What to build:** Extend support assistant for mixed inputs
- **Concepts introduced:** Multimodal model, Image input, Audio input, Video sampling, Multimodal RAG

#### Module 64: Local LLMs, Ollama and model routing — Days 316–320 (Advanced)
- **Goal / deliverable:** Run a local model and compare results
- **Why it matters:** Local models trade operating responsibility for greater control; route tasks based on measured requirements.
- **Simple .NET comparison:** You can use localhost model inference the way you call any internal HTTP service.
- **Example:** Cheap fast model for classification; stronger model for difficult reasoning.
- **What to build:** Run a local model and compare results
- **Concepts introduced:** Local inference, Ollama, Open-weight model, Model routing, Benchmark
- **Supports practical project:** `p16` — Local Model Comparison Lab
- **Common mistake to avoid:** Trying to memorize terms instead of running a tiny .NET experiment.
- **Official / external references:** [Ollama local-model runner](https://github.com/ollama/ollama) (`ollama`); [Microsoft.Extensions.AI — Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/microsoft-extensions-ai) (`mea`); [Microsoft Learn — AI for .NET](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/) (`aihome`)

**Existing C# starter illustration — Choose a provider using normal .NET DI:**
```csharp
public enum ModelTier { Local, FastCloud, ReasoningCloud }
public interface IModelSelector { ModelTier Select(string task); }
// Prefer a small, measurable routing policy; log quality and latency.
```
**Day-by-day tasks, explanations and proof:**

**Day 316: Local vs cloud** — *Understand*
- **Understand:** Local model means you manage hardware, upgrades and security.
- **Do:** Create comparison table for Azure hosted vs local inference.
- **Definition of done / proof:** Local/cloud tradeoff table
- **Read next:** [Ollama local-model runner](https://github.com/ollama/ollama) (`ollama`); [Microsoft.Extensions.AI — Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/microsoft-extensions-ai) (`mea`); [Microsoft Learn — AI for .NET](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/) (`aihome`)

**Day 317: Run Ollama optionally** — *Try a tiny example*
- **Understand:** Ollama runs supported open-weight models on suitable hardware.

- **Read next:** [Ollama local-model runner](https://github.com/ollama/ollama) (`ollama`); [Microsoft.Extensions.AI — Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/microsoft-extensions-ai) (`mea`); [Microsoft Learn — AI for .NET](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/) (`aihome`)

#### Module 65: When to use RAG vs fine-tuning vs training — Days 321–325 (Advanced)
- **Goal / deliverable:** Create a decision guide for model adaptation
- **Why it matters:** RAG supplies fresh facts; fine-tuning changes tendencies or behavior; new model training is a larger undertaking.
- **Simple .NET comparison:** RAG is querying a knowledge store; fine-tuning resembles modifying the learned program.
- **Example:** Company leave policy changes daily → RAG; stable structured format may be tuned.
- **What to build:** Create a decision guide for model adaptation
- **Concepts introduced:** Fine-tuning, RAG vs tuning, LoRA, QLoRA, Training data
- **Supports practical project:** `p16` — Local Model Comparison Lab
- **Common mistake to avoid:** Trying to memorize terms instead of running a tiny .NET experiment.
- **Official / external references:** [Microsoft Foundry documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/) (`foundry`); [Azure Machine Learning documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/machine-learning/) (`azureml`); [Generative AI for Beginners — .NET](https://github.com/microsoft/Generative-AI-for-beginners-dotnet) (`genai`)

**Existing C# starter illustration — Choose prompt, retrieval, tool or tuning:**
```csharp
Need current private facts?      -> RAG
Need real-time business data?  -> Tools / APIs
Need a precise output schema?  -> Structured output
Need stable behavior changes?  -> Consider fine-tuning
Need a numeric prediction?    -> Consider ML.NET
```

#### Module 66: Performance, ONNX, quantization and deeper ML paths — Days 326–330 (Advanced)
- **Goal / deliverable:** Create an inference-performance experiment
- **Why it matters:** AI performance depends on hardware, model representation, batching and inference libraries.
- **Simple .NET comparison:** Think of optimizing a database query: measure first, then optimize the bottleneck.
- **Example:** Quantization reduces weight precision; ONNX Runtime can execute compatible models in C#.
- **What to build:** Create an inference-performance experiment
- **Concepts introduced:** Quantization, ONNX Runtime, CPU/GPU, Latency, Throughput
- **Supports practical project:** `p16` — Local Model Comparison Lab
- **Common mistake to avoid:** Trying to memorize terms instead of running a tiny .NET experiment.
- **Official / external references:** [ONNX Runtime .NET](https://onnxruntime.ai/docs/get-started/with-csharp.html) (`onnx`); [ONNX Runtime repository](https://github.com/microsoft/onnxruntime) (`onnxrepo`); [Azure Machine Learning documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/machine-learning/) (`azureml`); [PyTorch — optional deep learning](https://pytorch.org/tutorials/) (`pytorch`)

**Existing C# starter illustration — Measure time before optimizing an inference path:**
```csharp
var clock = System.Diagnostics.Stopwatch.StartNew();
// Run one ONNX Runtime, API or local model inference here.
clock.Stop();
Console.WriteLine($"Inference took {clock.ElapsedMilliseconds}ms");
// Warmup, batching and actual model accuracy affect comparisons.
```

### PHASE 12: Secure, measure and ship an enterprise .NET AI platform — Days 331–360
#### Module 67: AI security, privacy and responsible system design — Days 331–335 (Advanced)
- **Goal / deliverable:** Threat-model all AI data and tool boundaries
- **Why it matters:** AI adds novel attack surfaces, but your regular .NET defenses still apply.
- **Simple .NET comparison:** Treat prompts, files, tools and model output as untrusted input.
- **Example:** A retrieved PDF might contain text asking to leak another tenant’s data.
- **What to build:** Threat-model all AI data and tool boundaries
- **Concepts introduced:** Prompt injection, Indirect injection, PII, Tenant isolation, Tool abuse
- **Supports practical project:** `p16` — Local Model Comparison Lab
- **Common mistake to avoid:** Trying to memorize terms instead of running a tiny .NET experiment.
- **Official / external references:** [OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications](https://genai.owasp.org/llm-top-10/) (`owasp`); [Microsoft Responsible AI resources](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/ai/responsible-ai) (`privacy`); [Microsoft Entra identity platform](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity-platform/) (`identity`)

**Existing C# starter illustration — Make authorization a deterministic requirement:**
```csharp
public interface IAuthorizedOrderService
{
    Task<OrderDto?> GetForCurrentUserAsync(int id, CancellationToken ct);
}
// Never let an LLM select a different user's TenantId.
```
**Day-by-day tasks, explanations and proof:**

**Day 331: Prompt injection vs normal injection** — *Understand*
- **Understand:** An attacker may place commands inside text given to the model.

- **Read next:** [OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications](https://genai.owasp.org/llm-top-10/) (`owasp`); [Microsoft Responsible AI resources](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/ai/responsible-ai) (`privacy`); [Microsoft Entra identity platform](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity-platform/) (`identity`)

#### Module 68: Test and evaluate chat, RAG and agents — Days 336–340 (Advanced)
- **Goal / deliverable:** Build your full AI evaluation suite
- **Why it matters:** The best AI regression testing combines deterministic checks, labeled evidence and human review.
- **Simple .NET comparison:** Write xUnit for exact C# behavior; score fuzzy model outputs with careful rubrics.
- **Example:** Answers can be relevant but unsupported, or grounded but incomplete.
- **What to build:** Build your full AI evaluation suite
- **Concepts introduced:** RAG evaluation, Groundedness, LLM-as-judge, Golden set, Red teaming
- **Supports practical project:** `p16` — Local Model Comparison Lab
- **Common mistake to avoid:** Trying to memorize terms instead of running a tiny .NET experiment.
- **Official / external references:** [Microsoft Learn — Retrieval-augmented generation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/search/retrieval-augmented-generation-overview) (`azrag`); [Microsoft Foundry documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/) (`foundry`); [Microsoft Agent Framework for .NET](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet) (`agent`)

**Existing C# starter illustration — Simple retrieval assertion in a unit test:**
```csharp
string[] retrieved = ["policy-7", "policy-2"];
string expected = "policy-7";
Console.WriteLine(retrieved.Take(2).Contains(expected)); // True
// Expand to Recall@k, groundedness checks and negative cases.
```

#### Module 69: Observability, cost, resilience and AI gateway — Days 341–345 (Advanced)
- **Goal / deliverable:** Instrument and harden your platform
- **Why it matters:** Operate AI as an external dependency with tracing, budgets, retries and measurable outcomes.
- **Simple .NET comparison:** ILogger + OpenTelemetry + Polly-style resilience concepts still apply.
- **Example:** Trace shows API → retrieval → model → tool → final response, including costs.
- **What to build:** Instrument and harden your platform
- **Concepts introduced:** OpenTelemetry, Tokens and cost, Rate limiting, Caching, Model gateway
- **Supports practical project:** `p16` — Local Model Comparison Lab
- **Common mistake to avoid:** Trying to memorize terms instead of running a tiny .NET experiment.
- **Official / external references:** [OpenTelemetry .NET](https://github.com/open-telemetry/opentelemetry-dotnet) (`otel`); [Microsoft Learn — Application Insights](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-monitor/app/app-insights-overview) (`appinsights`); [Microsoft.Extensions.AI — Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/microsoft-extensions-ai) (`mea`); [.NET Aspire](https://github.com/dotnet/aspire) (`aspire`)

**Existing C# starter illustration — Reliability checklist:**
```csharp
Validate request + identity + budget
  -> Retrieve only authorized data
  -> Call model with CancellationToken
  -> Trace tokens/latency/cost
  -> Validate tool args again before side effects
  -> Avoid retrying non-idempotent writes
  -> Fail safely if provider is unavailable
```
**Day-by-day tasks, explanations and proof:**

**Day 341: Trace the full request** — *Understand*
- **Understand:** Correlate HTTP, vector query, model call and tools using one trace ID.
- **Do:** Add ActivitySource/OpenTelemetry spans to the AI service.

- **Read next:** [OpenTelemetry .NET](https://github.com/open-telemetry/opentelemetry-dotnet) (`otel`); [Microsoft Learn — Application Insights](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-monitor/app/app-insights-overview) (`appinsights`); [Microsoft.Extensions.AI — Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/microsoft-extensions-ai) (`mea`)

#### Module 71: CI/CD, release gates, governance and feedback — Days 351–355 (Advanced)
- **Goal / deliverable:** Build an AI-aware .NET delivery pipeline
- **Why it matters:** Production AI needs versioned prompts/models/indexes and rollback just like web software.
- **Simple .NET comparison:** Your release pipeline checks code AND AI quality before promotion.
- **Example:** A new prompt that reduces Recall@5 or leaks data must block release.
- **What to build:** Build an AI-aware .NET delivery pipeline
- **Concepts introduced:** CI/CD, Feature flag, Rollback, AI quality gate, Governance
- **Supports practical project:** `p16` — Local Model Comparison Lab
- **Common mistake to avoid:** Trying to memorize terms instead of running a tiny .NET experiment.
- **Official / external references:** [Azure DevOps documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/devops/) (`azuredevops`); [Azure Developer CLI](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/developer/azure-developer-cli/overview) (`azd`); [Microsoft Learn — Application Insights](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-monitor/app/app-insights-overview) (`appinsights`); [OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications](https://genai.owasp.org/llm-top-10/) (`owasp`)

**Existing C# starter illustration — Stop a bad change from shipping:**
```csharp
double recallBefore = .91, recallAfter = .77;
if (recallAfter < recallBefore - .05)
    throw new InvalidOperationException("RAG regression: block release");
// Real CI gates combine security, quality, latency and cost thresholds.
```
**Day-by-day tasks, explanations and proof:**

**Day 351: Version everything** — *Understand*
- **Understand:** Code, prompt templates, vector index schema and evaluation sets are release assets.
- **Do:** Create version entries for prompt, model, embedding and index schema.
- **Definition of done / proof:** Release manifest
- **Read next:** [Azure DevOps documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/devops/) (`azuredevops`); [Azure Developer CLI](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/developer/azure-developer-cli/overview) (`azd`); [Microsoft Learn — Application Insights](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-monitor/app/app-insights-overview) (`appinsights`)

**Day 352: Automate builds and unit tests** — *Try a tiny example*
- **Understand:** Use familiar dotnet restore/build/test in CI.
- **Do:** Add a GitHub Actions or Azure DevOps pipeline for solution tests.
- **Definition of done / proof:** Green CI run
- **Read next:** [Azure DevOps documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/devops/) (`azuredevops`); [Azure Developer CLI](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/developer/azure-developer-cli/overview) (`azd`); [Microsoft Learn — Application Insights](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-monitor/app/app-insights-overview) (`appinsights`)

**Day 353: Add AI regression gates** — *Write C# / build*
- **Understand:** Golden set and security checks can fail a release.
- **Do:** Add a scripted quality threshold and tenant-isolation checks.
- **Definition of done / proof:** AI quality gate
- **Read next:** [Azure DevOps documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/devops/) (`azuredevops`); [Azure Developer CLI](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/developer/azure-developer-cli/overview) (`azd`); [Microsoft Learn — Application Insights](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-monitor/app/app-insights-overview) (`appinsights`)

**Day 354: Canary, feature flag and rollback** — *Test your understanding*
- **Understand:** Safely compare updates before all users receive them.
- **Do:** Write feature-flag rollout and rollback plan for a new model deployment.
- **Definition of done / proof:** Rollback playbook
- **Read next:** [Azure DevOps documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/devops/) (`azuredevops`); [Azure Developer CLI](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/developer/azure-developer-cli/overview) (`azd`); [Microsoft Learn — Application Insights](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-monitor/app/app-insights-overview) (`appinsights`)

**Day 355: Human feedback and governance** — *Finish and explain*
- **Understand:** Production quality requires feedback loops, documented limitations and audits.
- **Do:** Design thumbs-up/down with optional comment and a triage queue.
- **Definition of done / proof:** Feedback and governance plan
- **Read next:** [Azure DevOps documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/devops/) (`azuredevops`); [Azure Developer CLI](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/developer/azure-developer-cli/overview) (`azd`); [Microsoft Learn — Application Insights](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-monitor/app/app-insights-overview) (`appinsights`)

#### Module 72: Final capstone: Enterprise .NET AI Platform — Days 356–360 (Advanced)
- **Goal / deliverable:** Build the Enterprise .NET AI Platform
- **Why it matters:** Prove the skills by integrating only the components you understand and testing them end to end.
- **Simple .NET comparison:** This is a full-stack product, not a giant prompt: each feature has its own secure service boundary.
- **Example:** Ask company policy Q&A with citations, then open a case through an approved tool.
- **What to build:** Build the Enterprise .NET AI Platform
- **Concepts introduced:** Capstone, Full-stack AI system, RAG + agents, Evaluation, Azure release
- **Supports practical project:** `p17` — Enterprise .NET AI Platform
- **Common mistake to avoid:** Trying to memorize terms instead of running a tiny .NET experiment.
- **Official / external references:** [Azure RAG demo — ASP.NET + Blazor (C#)](https://github.com/Azure-Samples/azure-search-openai-demo-csharp) (`ragcsharp`); [Microsoft Agent Framework for .NET](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet) (`agent`); [Official MCP C# SDK](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/csharp-sdk) (`mcpgit`); [Microsoft Foundry documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/) (`foundry`)

**Existing C# starter illustration — Keep business interfaces separate from AI clients:**
```csharp
public interface IEnterpriseKnowledgeAssistant
{
    Task<RagResponse> AnswerAsync(string question, CancellationToken ct);
}
// Blazor -> ASP.NET API -> app service -> search/model -> citations.
```
**Day-by-day tasks, explanations and proof:**

**Day 356: Plan scope and application architecture** — *Understand*
- **Understand:** Decide which features are necessary and what can remain optional.
- **Do:** Draw solution layers: Blazor, ASP.NET API, AI services, SQL/Search/Blob, tools, telemetry.
- **Definition of done / proof:** Final architecture diagram

**Day 358: Integrate bounded agent + MCP** — *Write C# / build*

## Appendix C — Complete 18-project hands-on ladder
**Important:** The source provides instructions and acceptance criteria; full runnable source for each learner project is not bundled into the static academy ZIP. Create real sample code as a future improvement.

### Project 01: AI vs Rules Decision Notebook (`p0`, starts at Module 01)
- **Stage:** Beginner
- **Problem / why:** Your first practical exercise: decide what should remain normal C# code.
- **.NET/tool stack:** C# console • Markdown
- **Input:** 12 business feature requests
- **Expected output:** Decision table with reasons
- **Implementation sequence:**
  1. Create a console project and define 12 fake feature requests.
  2. Tag each request Rules, Predictive ML, Generative AI or Search.
  3. Write why some financial/security features must remain deterministic.
  4. Explain decisions in README with a diagram.
- **Acceptance / definition of done:** You can justify every choice without buzzwords.
- **Relevant references:** [Microsoft Learn — AI for .NET](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/) (`aihome`); [Microsoft AI for Beginners — conceptual](https://github.com/microsoft/AI-For-Beginners) (`aibeg`)


### Project 02: ML.NET Sentiment REST API (`p1`, starts at Module 15)
- **Stage:** Beginner
- **Problem / why:** Train, evaluate, save and serve your first real C# machine-learning model.
- **.NET/tool stack:** C# • ML.NET • ASP.NET Core
- **Input:** Customer review text
- **Expected output:** Positive/Negative prediction + score
- **Implementation sequence:**
  1. Prepare 60 fake labeled reviews; remove duplicates and hidden labels.
  2. Train and evaluate a binary classifier from official ML.NET C# sample.
  3. Store the model file and serve POST /api/sentiment.
  4. Test empty input, surprising text, and confusion-matrix metrics.
- **Acceptance / definition of done:** Tests pass; model is trained offline, loaded for inference, and its limitations are documented.
- **Relevant references:** [ML.NET sentiment classifier (C#)](https://github.com/dotnet/machinelearning-samples/tree/main/samples/csharp/getting-started/BinaryClassification_SentimentAnalysis) (`mlsentiment`); [Microsoft Learn — ML.NET classification](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/machine-learning/tutorials/sentiment-analysis) (`mldocs`)


### Project 03: Delivery ETA Predictor (`p2`, starts at Module 16)
- **Stage:** Beginner
- **Problem / why:** Try regression so AI is not just chatbots and classification.
- **.NET/tool stack:** C# • ML.NET • Web API
- **Input:** Distance, weight, order size
- **Expected output:** Estimated delivery days
- **Implementation sequence:**
  1. Create a small synthetic delivery dataset and numeric features.
  2. Train simple regression and report MAE vs average baseline.
  3. Add POST /api/estimate-delivery.
  4. Document prediction uncertainty and validate strange inputs.
- **Acceptance / definition of done:** Working estimate endpoint with honest error table.
- **Relevant references:** [ML.NET official sample repository](https://github.com/dotnet/machinelearning-samples) (`mlrepo`); [Microsoft Learn — ML.NET](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/machine-learning/) (`mlguide`)


### Project 04: C# Console LLM Chat (`p3`, starts at Module 26)
- **Stage:** Beginner
- **Problem / why:** One secure model API call becomes a streamed multi-turn conversation.
- **.NET/tool stack:** C# • official model .NET SDK
- **Input:** A typed question
- **Expected output:** Streamed model response
- **Implementation sequence:**
  1. Store provider and deployment config outside source control.
  2. Call a model once and print a response (stub first if no provider).
  3. Append conversation history; use explicit message roles.
  4. Add streaming, cancellation and basic token/time logging.
- **Acceptance / definition of done:** You can explain what was sent, returned, billed, and how you handle failures.
- **Relevant references:** [OpenAI official .NET SDK](https://github.com/openai/openai-dotnet) (`openai`); [Generative AI for Beginners — .NET](https://github.com/microsoft/Generative-AI-for-beginners-dotnet) (`genai`)


### Project 05: Blazor Chat UI (`p4`, starts at Module 30)
- **Stage:** Beginner
- **Problem / why:** Turn your console example into a familiar ASP.NET Core + Blazor app.
- **.NET/tool stack:** Blazor • ASP.NET Core • DI
- **Input:** Browser chat message
- **Expected output:** Safe streamed conversation
- **Implementation sequence:**
  1. Register a server-side IAssistantService and DTOs.
  2. Implement protected chat endpoint and connect Blazor form.
  3. Display streaming, loading, error and Cancel states.
  4. Write tests for blank input and failed model calls.
- **Acceptance / definition of done:** No API keys in browser; errors and cancellations work.
- **Relevant references:** [Microsoft Learn — ASP.NET Core Blazor](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/blazor/) (`blazor`); [Generative AI for Beginners — .NET](https://github.com/microsoft/Generative-AI-for-beginners-dotnet) (`genai`)


### Project 06: Structured Text Intelligence Playground (`p5`, starts at Module 34)
- **Stage:** Beginner
- **Problem / why:** Classify, summarize, translate and extract typed ticket details.
- **.NET/tool stack:** ASP.NET Core • Blazor • JSON Schema
- **Input:** “Invoice INV-22 has failed”
- **Expected output:** TicketInfo JSON + optional summary
- **Implementation sequence:**
  1. Define typed records, nullable fields and allowed priority enum.
  2. Implement schema-constrained extraction with separate C# validation.
  3. Add Blazor tabs/actions for extract, summarize and classify.
  4. Run 20 cases including missing fields and injection attempts.
- **Acceptance / definition of done:** Invalid JSON and missing data are handled without made-up values.
- **Relevant references:** [OpenAI official .NET SDK](https://github.com/openai/openai-dotnet) (`openai`); [.NET AI samples](https://github.com/dotnet/ai-samples) (`aisamples`)


### Project 07: AI Order Tool Assistant (`p6`, starts at Module 39)
- **Stage:** Intermediate
- **Problem / why:** Teach an LLM to request safe .NET functions instead of guessing business data.
- **.NET/tool stack:** C# • SQLite • function calling
- **Input:** “Has order 4521 shipped?”
- **Expected output:** Verified order status
- **Implementation sequence:**
  1. Seed 10 orders with customer ownership IDs.
  2. Describe GetOrder and GetShipmentStatus tool schemas.
  3. Validate ID and authorize the current user before service calls.
  4. Add max steps, trace logs, no-side-effect policy and negative tests.
- **Acceptance / definition of done:** Another user’s order cannot be read; tool outputs reflect SQL truth.
- **Relevant references:** [OpenAI official .NET SDK](https://github.com/openai/openai-dotnet) (`openai`); [Microsoft Learn — Agent Framework course](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/agent-framework/get-started/) (`agentstart`)


### Project 08: Semantic Product Search (`p7`, starts at Module 41)
- **Stage:** Intermediate
- **Problem / why:** See meaning-based matching without needing any vector database.
- **.NET/tool stack:** C# • float[] • embeddings • LINQ
- **Input:** “headset for work calls”
- **Expected output:** Relevant top-5 products
- **Implementation sequence:**
  1. Create 60 fake product descriptions.
  2. Generate embeddings once and store them with model/version IDs.
  3. Implement cosine similarity by hand and sort with LINQ.
  4. Compare 10 semantic queries against keyword search.
- **Acceptance / definition of done:** Top-5 matches make sense and mismatched dimensions are rejected.
- **Relevant references:** [Microsoft.Extensions.AI — Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/microsoft-extensions-ai) (`mea`); [Generative AI for Beginners — .NET](https://github.com/microsoft/Generative-AI-for-beginners-dotnet) (`genai`)


### Project 09: Vector Search API (`p8`, starts at Module 42)
- **Stage:** Intermediate
- **Problem / why:** Upgrade semantic search to PostgreSQL+pgvector or Qdrant.
- **.NET/tool stack:** ASP.NET Core • pgvector/Qdrant
- **Input:** Search + TenantId + Category
- **Expected output:** Top-k filtered results
- **Implementation sequence:**
  1. Set up a local vector store and metadata schema.
  2. Insert existing product vectors without re-embedding per request.
  3. Query nearest neighbors and add indexes and filters.
  4. Test tenant isolation, deletions and latency.
- **Acceptance / definition of done:** Search never returns outside its allowed tenant/category.
- **Relevant references:** [pgvector-dotnet](https://github.com/pgvector/pgvector-dotnet) (`pgvec`); [Qdrant .NET client](https://github.com/qdrant/qdrant-dotnet) (`qdrant`)


### Project 10: Basic Document RAG Assistant (`p9`, starts at Module 48)
- **Stage:** Intermediate
- **Problem / why:** Upload policies, find relevant passages and answer with real citations.
- **.NET/tool stack:** Blazor • ASP.NET Core • vectors • PDF
- **Input:** “What is our refund period?”
- **Expected output:** Answer + document/page provenance
- **Implementation sequence:**
  1. Ingest 3 fake PDF/DOCX files with source and page metadata.
  2. Chunk, embed and store document fragments.
  3. Search allowed fragments and assemble evidence in a prompt.
  4. Return answer or “not found” with clickable source preview.
- **Acceptance / definition of done:** 10 known questions cite real sources; unknown questions are not invented.
- **Relevant references:** [Azure RAG demo — ASP.NET + Blazor (C#)](https://github.com/Azure-Samples/azure-search-openai-demo-csharp) (`ragcsharp`); [Microsoft Learn — Retrieval-augmented generation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/search/retrieval-augmented-generation-overview) (`azrag`)


### Project 11: RAG Quality Test Harness (`p10`, starts at Module 49)
- **Stage:** Intermediate
- **Problem / why:** Build reusable quality measurement for retrieval and grounded answers.
- **.NET/tool stack:** xUnit • C# • CSV/JSON fixtures
- **Input:** Golden dataset + RAG results
- **Expected output:** Recall@k + answer quality report
- **Implementation sequence:**
  1. Label 50 questions with allowed sources and expected answer facts.
  2. Implement Recall@k/Precision@k and error reports in C#.
  3. Score groundedness on 10 manually audited cases.
  4. Make one regression gate fail for intentionally bad retrieval.
- **Acceptance / definition of done:** RAG changes have visible measurable pass/fail criteria.
- **Relevant references:** [Microsoft Learn — Retrieval-augmented generation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/search/retrieval-augmented-generation-overview) (`azrag`); [Microsoft Foundry documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/) (`foundry`)


### Project 12: Advanced Enterprise RAG (`p11`, starts at Module 53)
- **Stage:** Intermediate
- **Problem / why:** Improve recall, freshness, hybrid search, authorization and tests.
- **.NET/tool stack:** Azure AI Search • C# • Blazor
- **Input:** Multi-document employee question
- **Expected output:** Access-safe evidence-backed reply
- **Implementation sequence:**
  1. Create 30–50 labelled retrieval cases.
  2. Add keyword+vector hybrid, reranking and query rewriting.
  3. Filter authorized TenantId before retrieval; test cross-tenant attacks.
  4. Implement document update/delete/reindex and measure quality/cost.
- **Acceptance / definition of done:** A test report proves both retrieval improvement and tenant isolation.
- **Relevant references:** [Azure RAG demo — ASP.NET + Blazor (C#)](https://github.com/Azure-Samples/azure-search-openai-demo-csharp) (`ragcsharp`); [Microsoft Learn — Hybrid search](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/search/hybrid-search-overview) (`azhybrid`)


### Project 13: Customer Support Agent (`p12`, starts at Module 57)
- **Stage:** Intermediate
- **Problem / why:** Use Agent Framework to combine lookup, RAG and approval safely.
- **.NET/tool stack:** Microsoft Agent Framework • SQL • RAG
- **Input:** “My delivery arrived damaged”
- **Expected output:** Pending case / approved operation
- **Implementation sequence:**
  1. Run numbered C# Agent Framework get-started samples.
  2. Reuse GetOrderStatus and SearchKnowledgeBase as tools.
  3. Add session state, step limit and human approval for write tools.
  4. Verify unknown order, cross-tenant and timeout paths.
- **Acceptance / definition of done:** Agent stops predictably and cannot bypass backend authorization.
- **Relevant references:** [Microsoft Learn — Agent Framework course](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/agent-framework/get-started/) (`agentstart`); [Agent Framework — numbered .NET samples](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet/samples/01-get-started) (`agentsamples`)


### Project 14: Researcher + Reviewer Workflow (`p13`, starts at Module 59)
- **Stage:** Intermediate
- **Problem / why:** Compare a two-agent review process with a simpler one-agent baseline.
- **.NET/tool stack:** Microsoft Agent Framework • ASP.NET Core
- **Input:** “Compare two Azure hosting options”
- **Expected output:** Reviewed answer with evidence
- **Implementation sequence:**
  1. Make a researcher agent gather evidence and a reviewer score it.
  2. Add a parallel information-collection stage.
  3. Limit revision loops to two rounds and record results.
  4. Compare quality, latency, token usage and source correctness to single agent.
- **Acceptance / definition of done:** All stage transitions are explicit and auditable.
- **Relevant references:** [Agent Framework — .NET workflows](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet/samples/03-workflows) (`agentworkflow`); [Microsoft Agent Framework for .NET](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet) (`agent`)


### Project 15: C# MCP Server and Client (`p14`, starts at Module 60)
- **Stage:** Intermediate
- **Problem / why:** Expose selected C# business tools over a common protocol.
- **.NET/tool stack:** .NET • MCP C# SDK • auth
- **Input:** get_order_status tool call
- **Expected output:** Structured JSON order response
- **Implementation sequence:**
  1. Build MCP server exposing safe get_order_status and search_policy.
  2. Expose one resource/prompt and document tool schemas.
  3. Connect a C# MCP client over supported transport.
  4. Enforce caller permissions; test unknown tool and invalid ID.
- **Acceptance / definition of done:** MCP discovery works and unauthorized calls do not.
- **Relevant references:** [Official MCP C# SDK](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/csharp-sdk) (`mcpgit`); [Microsoft Learn — Get started with .NET MCP](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/get-started-mcp) (`mcplearn`)


### Project 16: Invoice + Voice Mini Assistant (`p15`, starts at Module 62)
- **Stage:** Advanced
- **Problem / why:** Work with OCR, typed invoice extraction and a small speech workflow.
- **.NET/tool stack:** Blazor • Document Intelligence • Speech SDK
- **Input:** Invoice image / voice note
- **Expected output:** Typed invoice / transcript
- **Implementation sequence:**
  1. Extract key invoice fields and keep field provenance.
  2. Validate totals and missing values with deterministic C#.
  3. Add voice-note transcription and optional spoken confirmation.
  4. Show human-review state on low-confidence fields.
- **Acceptance / definition of done:** Users can correct extracted fields; data is not treated as infallible.
- **Relevant references:** [Azure AI Document Intelligence](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/document-intelligence/) (`docintel`); [Azure Speech service](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/speech-service/) (`speech`)


### Project 17: Local Model Comparison Lab (`p16`, starts at Module 64)
- **Stage:** Advanced
- **Problem / why:** Run a local supported model if feasible and compare against a cloud model.
- **.NET/tool stack:** C# • Ollama • IChatClient
- **Input:** 10 test prompts
- **Expected output:** Quality/latency comparison
- **Implementation sequence:**
  1. Identify local hardware constraints and optional Ollama install.
  2. Call local endpoint from C# with config-only switching.
  3. Run 10 same cases against available models.
  4. Document performance, memory, safety and privacy tradeoffs.
- **Acceptance / definition of done:** Evidence-based model choice; fully documented fallback if hardware cannot run it.
- **Relevant references:** [Ollama local-model runner](https://github.com/ollama/ollama) (`ollama`); [Microsoft.Extensions.AI — Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/ai/microsoft-extensions-ai) (`mea`)


### Project 18: Enterprise .NET AI Platform (`p17`, starts at Module 72)
- **Stage:** Advanced
- **Problem / why:** Final integration: secure RAG, tool assistant, agent approval, evaluations and Azure operations.
- **.NET/tool stack:** Blazor • ASP.NET Core • Microsoft Foundry • Azure AI Search
- **Input:** Policies + customer/order support task
- **Expected output:** Verified responses, sources, tools and telemetry
- **Implementation sequence:**
  1. Create layered Blazor/ASP.NET architecture and safe config.
  2. Integrate document ingestion, hybrid RAG and citations.
  3. Connect authorized tools, bounded agent, MCP and approval step.
  4. Run RAG evaluation, security/tenant tests and OpenTelemetry traces.
  5. Deploy to staging with azd/Bicep, budgets and CI/CD rollback.
  6. Publish README, architecture, tests, demo and residual risks.
- **Acceptance / definition of done:** A stranger can reproduce the system, evaluate quality and see safe operation from logs.
- **Relevant references:** [Azure RAG demo — ASP.NET + Blazor (C#)](https://github.com/Azure-Samples/azure-search-openai-demo-csharp) (`ragcsharp`); [Microsoft Agent Framework for .NET](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet) (`agent`); [Official MCP C# SDK](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/csharp-sdk) (`mcpgit`); [Microsoft Foundry documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/) (`foundry`)
