## Appendix E — All 208 glossary definitions
Terms are preserved verbatim from the existing glossary. Categorization is for browsing only; these definitions are teaching aids, not a substitute for full API/documentation coverage.

### .NET Engineering
- **BackgroundService:** .NET abstraction for running long-running hosted background work.
- **CancellationToken:** .NET mechanism for cooperatively cancelling async work.
- **Dependency Injection:** Supplying service dependencies via constructors or .NET service container.
- **DTO:** Data Transfer Object, e.g. a C# record for request or response data.
- **IAsyncEnumerable:** .NET abstraction for asynchronously streaming a sequence of values.
- **IChatClient:** Microsoft.Extensions.AI interface for a chat-model client.
- **IDataView:** ML.NET tabular data abstraction for loading and transforming rows.
- **IEmbeddingGenerator:** Microsoft.Extensions.AI interface family for embedding generation.
- **JSON Schema:** Document defining the fields, types and constraints of JSON content.
- **Middleware:** Processing layer around a request or operation, often adding logs or policies.
- **MLContext:** Entry point for ML.NET training and evaluation operations.
- **SSE:** Server-Sent Events; one-way HTTP stream used for incremental updates.
- **User secrets:** Development-time way to keep secrets outside committed configuration files.

### Advanced
- **Fine-tuning with LoRA:** Low-rank adaptation that trains relatively few additional parameters.
- **GraphRAG:** Combining graph-like relationships with retrieval and generation.
- **KV cache:** Inference technique reusing attention key/value computation across generated tokens.
- **LoRA:** Training method that updates small low-rank adapters rather than all base weights.
- **Model distillation:** Training a smaller model to mimic useful behavior of a larger model.
- **ONNX:** Interchange format/ecosystem for representing and serving machine learning models.
- **QLoRA:** Low-rank tuning combined with quantized model weights.
- **Quantization precision:** Numeric bit-width used for model weights and calculations.

### Agents
- **A2A:** Agent-to-agent communication approach/protocol family; useful for cross-agent interoperability.
- **A2A protocol:** Agent-to-agent communication protocol, distinct from MCP tool access.
- **Agent:** Application-controlled process that uses a model, tools and state to pursue a goal.
- **Agent harness:** Runtime that coordinates tools, state, instructions, planning and safety.
- **Agent session:** Logical conversation/task context maintained for an agent.
- **Approval gate:** Rule requiring an authorized person to approve a sensitive operation.
- **Episodic memory:** Saved summaries of previous interactions/events for later retrieval.
- **Human-in-the-loop:** Human approval/review required before certain actions or decisions.
- **Idempotency:** Repeating an operation has no extra side effect.
- **Long-term preference:** Persistent user setting stored with explicit retention/control.
- **Max-steps budget:** Hard cap on number of agent turns/tool executions.
- **MCP:** Model Context Protocol, for exposing tools and context to compatible AI clients.
- **MCP host:** Application running an MCP client connection on behalf of a user/agent.
- **MCP prompt:** Reusable prompt template exposed by an MCP server.
- **MCP resource:** Readable context data exposed by an MCP server.
- **MCP transport:** Communication method for MCP, e.g. standard I/O or HTTP.
- **Orchestrator:** Code coordinating agent or workflow steps and state transitions.
- **State compaction:** Compressing context or old steps to fit limited model context.
- **Tool / function calling:** Model requests an application-defined operation; application validates and executes it.
- **Tool schema:** Definition of tool name, description and allowed arguments.
- **Workflow checkpoint:** Persisted state allowing an operation to resume after interruptions.
- **Working memory:** Temporary facts/state used within a current task or workflow.

### Azure
- **Azure AI Search:** Azure service for vector, keyword, hybrid and semantic search.
- **Azure Application Insights:** Azure telemetry service for app metrics, logs, traces and availability.
- **Azure Developer CLI (azd):** CLI for provisioning, deploying and removing app infrastructure.
- **Azure Document Intelligence:** Azure service for extracting text/layout/fields from documents.
- **Azure Key Vault:** Azure service managing secrets, keys and certificates.
- **Bicep:** Declarative language for defining Azure infrastructure resources.
- **Microsoft Entra ID:** Identity platform for users, service principals and access control.
- **Microsoft Foundry:** Azure platform for building, deploying, evaluating and governing AI solutions.

### Deep Learning
- **Activation:** Function that allows a neural network layer to model nonlinear behavior.
- **Backpropagation:** Computing how each trainable weight contributed to the model error.
- **Batch:** Smaller group of training examples processed together.
- **Epoch:** One pass over the full training dataset.
- **Gradient:** Direction and rate of loss change for a model parameter.
- **Learning rate:** Size of each parameter update while training.
- **Loss:** Number representing how wrong a model prediction is during training.
- **Neuron:** Small mathematical unit that combines weighted inputs then applies a function.
- **Optimizer:** Algorithm used to adjust model parameters based on gradients.
- **Positional encoding:** Information that helps transformer know token order.
- **ReLU:** Simple activation returning max(0,x).
- **Self-attention:** Mechanism where positions in one sequence use information from other positions.

### Evaluation
- **Confidence calibration:** Checking whether prediction confidence aligns with observed accuracy.
- **Golden dataset:** Curated test examples with expected behavior/evidence.
- **Ground-truth label:** Known expected answer for an evaluation example.
- **Groundedness evaluation:** Checking whether generated claims are supported by given context.
- **LLM-as-judge:** Using a model to score model output; useful but can be inconsistent/biased.
- **MRR:** Mean Reciprocal Rank; rewards placing the first relevant result high.
- **nDCG:** Metric that considers relevance grades and result ranking positions.
- **Precision@k:** Fraction of retrieved top-k items that are relevant.
- **Recall@k:** Fraction of expected relevant items found among the top k results.
- **Regression gate:** Automated check that blocks changes making quality/security worse.

### Foundations
- **AI:** Umbrella term for systems performing tasks associated with intelligent behavior.
- **Classification:** Predicting a category such as spam/not spam.
- **Clustering:** Grouping similar data without predefined labels.
- **Data leakage:** Accidentally giving a model future/private/test information during training or retrieval.
- **Dataset:** A collection of examples used to train, validate or test a model.
- **Deep learning:** ML methods using multi-layer neural networks.
- **Deterministic:** Same valid inputs and state produce the same result every time.
- **Discriminative model:** A model primarily predicting labels, scores or boundaries rather than creating new content.
- **Feature:** Input variable that helps a model make a prediction.
- **Generative AI:** Models creating new text, images, audio, code or other content.
- **Generative model:** A model that creates text, audio, images, code or similar content.
- **Gradient descent:** Optimization process that repeatedly adjusts weights to reduce loss.
- **Inference:** Using a trained model to make a prediction or generate output.
- **Label:** Target answer associated with training examples.
- **Loss function:** Number indicating how wrong a model’s prediction is during training.
- **Machine learning (ML):** Models learn statistical patterns from data instead of following only hand-written rules.
- **Neural network:** Layers of numerical transformations whose parameters can be trained.
- **Overfitting:** Memorizing training patterns and failing to generalize to new inputs.
- **Planning / search:** Choosing sequences of actions or paths under goals and constraints.
- **Probabilistic:** Output involves uncertainty; a model can be wrong even when fluent.
- **Recommendation:** Ranking or suggesting items a person may like.
- **Regression:** Predicting a numeric value such as next month’s revenue.
- **Reinforcement learning:** Learning policies through rewards for actions in an environment.
- **Tensor:** Multi-dimensional collection of numerical values used by neural networks.
- **Test set:** Held-out data used for final unbiased performance estimates.
- **Training:** Updating model parameters using data and an objective.
- **Validation set:** Held-out data used to tune model decisions.

### LLMs
- **Attention:** Mechanism for weighting relationships among input positions.
- **Context engineering:** Selecting relevant instructions, state, tools and evidence for a model request.
- **Context window:** Maximum tokens that a model can consider in a request.
- **Few-shot:** Providing examples alongside an instruction to show desired behavior.
- **Fine-tuning:** Additional training to adapt a pretrained model.
- **Hallucination:** Model output that sounds plausible but is unsupported or false.
- **LLM:** Large language model trained to predict/generate language tokens.
- **Logits:** Raw numerical scores for possible outputs before probabilities are computed.
- **Model deployment:** Named model endpoint/configuration used by the application.
- **Nucleus sampling:** Top-p sampling: selecting from tokens within a cumulative probability pool.
- **Parameters / weights:** Learned numerical values inside a model.
- **Pretraining:** Large-scale initial training that teaches general representations/patterns.
- **Prompt:** Inputs and instructions sent to a generative model.
- **Prompt hierarchy:** Different message roles carry different instruction authority.
- **Rate-limit response (429):** API response indicating too many requests in a time window.
- **Schema constrained output:** Model output guided/limited to satisfy a specified data shape.
- **Softmax:** Function turning scores into a probability-like distribution.
- **Structured output:** Response constrained to an expected schema, usually JSON.
- **System message:** Higher-priority application instruction sent to a chat model.
- **Temperature:** Sampling control that usually changes response randomness.
- **Token:** Model input/output unit; not necessarily a word or character.
- **Tokenizer:** System for mapping text into token IDs and back.
- **Top-p:** Sampling mechanism that restricts choices by cumulative probability.
- **Transformer:** Neural architecture built around attention mechanisms.

### Machine Learning
- **Anomaly detection:** Finding observations that differ unusually from expected patterns.
- **Baseline:** Simple comparison solution used to prove whether more complexity helps.
- **Bias-variance tradeoff:** The tension between overly simple models and models too sensitive to training data.
- **Calibration:** Whether a prediction score like 0.8 corresponds to roughly 80% event frequency.
- **Class imbalance:** Some labels occur far more often than others.
- **Cold start:** Recommender challenge when a new user/item has little interaction data.
- **Concept drift:** Relationship between inputs and correct outputs changes over time.
- **Confusion matrix:** Table counting true/false positives and true/false negatives.
- **Data drift:** Input patterns change compared with what a model saw previously.
- **F1 score:** Harmonic mean balancing precision and recall.
- **False negative:** Model misses a condition that is actually present.
- **False positive:** Model predicts a condition present when it is actually absent.
- **Feature engineering:** Converting raw inputs into useful numeric or categorical model features.
- **Generalization:** How well a model handles examples beyond the training set.
- **K-means:** Clustering algorithm that groups data around learned centers.
- **MAE:** Mean absolute error; average absolute amount a numeric prediction misses by.
- **RMSE:** Root mean squared error; penalizes larger numeric mistakes more strongly.
- **ROC-AUC:** Metric measuring class-ranking quality over different classification thresholds.
- **Supervised learning:** Learn from examples that include the correct target label.
- **Time-series forecast:** Predicting future values based on an ordered history.
- **Train-test split:** Dividing examples into learning and unseen evaluation portions.
- **Unsupervised learning:** Look for patterns or groups without predefined labels.
- **Validation split:** Dataset portion used for comparing models or tuning decisions before final test.

### Math made easy
- **Distribution:** How values or probabilities are spread over possible outcomes.
- **Dot product:** Multiply corresponding vector elements and add the products.
- **Euclidean distance:** Straight-line distance between points/vectors in a numeric space.
- **Mean:** Arithmetic average of a set of values.
- **Median:** Middle value after sorting numbers.
- **Normalization:** Adjusting numerical representations onto a comparable scale.
- **Sample size:** Number of observations used in an experiment or estimate.
- **Scalar:** A single number such as 2.5.
- **Vector dimension:** Number of elements in a vector, e.g., 3 for [1,2,3].
- **Vector norm:** A measure of vector size or length.

### Multimodal
- **OCR:** Optical character recognition; reading text from scanned images.
- **Speech-to-text:** Converting spoken audio into text.
- **Text-to-speech:** Generating spoken audio from text.
- **Voice activity detection:** Detecting when speech starts/stops in audio input.

### Production
- **Canary release:** Limited rollout to detect problems before broad deployment.
- **Circuit breaker:** Pattern for failing fast when a dependency is repeatedly unavailable.
- **Feature flag:** Switch enabling/disabling a change for selected users.
- **Infrastructure as code:** Version-controlled description of cloud resources and configuration.
- **Local inference:** Running a model on hardware you operate rather than an external API.
- **Managed identity:** Azure identity for services that avoids application-managed long-lived secrets.
- **Model gateway:** Service choosing model/provider and applying common policy/telemetry.
- **Model routing:** Choosing among models based on task requirements and constraints.
- **Observability:** Logs, traces and metrics showing what the system did and why.
- **OpenTelemetry:** Open standard and SDK ecosystem for traces, metrics and logs.
- **P95 latency:** Time below which 95% of measured requests complete.
- **Quantization:** Using reduced numerical precision to lower model memory/compute requirements.
- **Rate limit:** Restriction on requests or tokens per unit time.
- **Rollback:** Returning to an earlier known-good configuration or release.
- **Token budget:** Limit for prompt+output tokens to control cost and context use.

### Search & RAG
- **ANN / HNSW:** Approximate nearest-neighbor search and a common graph-based index technique.
- **BM25:** Keyword-based document ranking method.
- **Chunk overlap:** Repeating boundary content between neighboring chunks to preserve context.
- **Chunking:** Splitting documents into searchable pieces.
- **Citation:** Reference allowing a reader to verify a claim against a source.
- **Cosine similarity:** Measure of how closely two vectors point in the same direction.
- **Embedding:** Numerical representation of data designed to capture useful relationships.
- **Embedding model:** Model turning text or other data into embedding vectors.
- **Grounding:** Basing an answer on specific trusted/retrieved evidence.
- **Hybrid search:** Combines lexical matching with vector similarity search.
- **Index freshness:** Whether indexed documents still match current source data.
- **Metadata filtering:** Restricting search to allowed categories, documents or tenants.
- **Multi-hop retrieval:** Combining information across multiple facts/sources to answer one question.
- **Parent-child retrieval:** Finding a small chunk then retrieving its larger parent section for context.
- **Provenance:** Source identity and traceability of evidence in a generated answer.
- **Query decomposition:** Splitting a complex question into smaller answerable questions.
- **Query rewriting:** Reformulating a user's query to improve retrieval precision.
- **RAG:** Retrieval-Augmented Generation: retrieve relevant evidence and provide it to a model.
- **Reranker:** Second-stage model that reorders retrieved candidates for a specific query.
- **RRF:** Reciprocal Rank Fusion; merging ranked result lists by their ranks.
- **Semantic chunking:** Splitting text based on topic or meaning boundaries.
- **Top-k:** Number of highest-ranked results returned by a retrieval system.
- **Vector database:** Database supporting storage and similarity search over vectors.

### Security
- **Access control list (ACL):** Document or resource permissions specifying who may access it.
- **Indirect prompt injection:** Malicious instructions embedded in retrieved documents/tool results/web pages.
- **Least privilege:** Granting only the permissions required for a task.
- **Model card:** Short record of model purpose, metrics, data limits and risks.
- **PII:** Personally identifiable information requiring appropriate privacy protections.
- **PII redaction:** Removing or masking sensitive personal information in logs/data.
- **Prompt injection:** Attempt to manipulate model behavior using untrusted instructions.
- **Risk register:** List of identified system risks, mitigations and remaining exposure.
- **Tenant isolation:** Ensuring one tenant cannot access another tenant’s data or state.

## Appendix F — All 12 optional advanced pathways
These are optional specialization routes, **not** prerequisites for the core 360-session .NET AI application engineering curriculum.

### Elective 01: Deeper classical ML with ML.NET
- **Track:** Data science without changing your backend
- **Why:** Train and evaluate tabular classification, regression, anomaly and recommendation models in C#, including feature pipelines.
- **Try:** Implement 2 baseline ML.NET models and compare leakage, precision/recall and MAE.
- **References:** [Microsoft Learn — ML.NET](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/machine-learning/) (`mlguide`); [ML.NET official sample repository](https://github.com/dotnet/machinelearning-samples) (`mlrepo`)

### Elective 02: Advanced search & GraphRAG
- **Track:** Knowledge and retrieval architecture
- **Why:** Explore hierarchical documents, entity relationships, graph traversal and complex multi-hop questions.
- **Try:** Model Customer → Order → Product → Warranty and compare it with vector-only lookup.
- **References:** [Microsoft GraphRAG — advanced elective](https://github.com/microsoft/graphrag) (`graphrag`); [Microsoft Learn — Retrieval-augmented generation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/search/retrieval-augmented-generation-overview) (`azrag`)

### Elective 03: MLOps with Azure Machine Learning
- **Track:** Advanced operations
- **Why:** Learn model registry, feature/data lineage, model validation, drift checks, re-training and deployment rollout.
- **Try:** Register and version one trained ML.NET or compatible ONNX artifact and prepare an update plan.
- **References:** [Azure Machine Learning documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/machine-learning/) (`azureml`); [ML.NET official sample repository](https://github.com/dotnet/machinelearning-samples) (`mlrepo`)

### Elective 04: Deep learning mathematics
- **Track:** Optional research depth
- **Why:** Expand from weighted sums into matrix multiplication, derivatives, gradients, Adam and attention equations.
- **Try:** Calculate one tiny gradient update by hand; verify using a small C# console script.
- **References:** [Microsoft AI for Beginners — conceptual](https://github.com/microsoft/AI-For-Beginners) (`aibeg`); [PyTorch — optional deep learning](https://pytorch.org/tutorials/) (`pytorch`)

### Elective 05: Transformer training and tokenizers
- **Track:** Optional research depth
- **Why:** Explore pretraining corpora, tokenization algorithms, sequence modeling, SFT, preference optimization and alignment.
- **Try:** Diagram a transformer forward pass and prepare an honest inference-vs-training comparison.
- **References:** [Generative AI for Beginners — .NET](https://github.com/microsoft/Generative-AI-for-beginners-dotnet) (`genai`); [PyTorch — optional deep learning](https://pytorch.org/tutorials/) (`pytorch`)

### Elective 06: Quantization and inference performance
- **Track:** Model serving
- **Why:** Understand FP32/FP16/INT8, KV caching, batching, distillation, CPU/GPU tradeoffs and ONNX optimization.
- **Try:** Measure latency and memory while scoring a small ONNX model using C#.
- **References:** [ONNX Runtime .NET](https://onnxruntime.ai/docs/get-started/with-csharp.html) (`onnx`); [Ollama local-model runner](https://github.com/ollama/ollama) (`ollama`)

### Elective 07: Advanced agent architecture
- **Track:** Agent systems
- **Why:** Explore middleware, checkpointing, workflows, distributed execution, protocols, bounded autonomy and permissions.
- **Try:** Create a durable support workflow and compare safety and token cost with one model call.
- **References:** [Agent Framework — .NET workflows](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/tree/main/dotnet/samples/03-workflows) (`agentworkflow`); [Microsoft Learn — Agent Framework course](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/agent-framework/get-started/) (`agentstart`)

### Elective 08: Recommenders and ranking
- **Track:** ML search specialist
- **Why:** Study collaborative filtering, cold start, learning-to-rank, offline ranking metrics and click feedback loops.
- **Try:** Recommend similar products with ML.NET and measure precision@K against popularity baseline.
- **References:** [ML.NET official sample repository](https://github.com/dotnet/machinelearning-samples) (`mlrepo`); [Microsoft Learn — Hybrid search](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/search/hybrid-search-overview) (`azhybrid`)

### Elective 09: Multimodal and image generation
- **Track:** Optional media specialist
- **Why:** Explore multimodal embeddings, image generation, object detection, segmentation and vision safety.
- **Try:** Build a C# API processing a product image plus text, with deterministic metadata validation.
- **References:** [Azure AI Vision](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/computer-vision/) (`vision`); [Azure AI Document Intelligence](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/document-intelligence/) (`docintel`)

### Elective 10: Real-time voice and streaming
- **Track:** Multimodal specialist
- **Why:** Go deeper into streaming speech APIs, turn detection, interruptions, diarization and response latency.
- **Try:** Measure voice turn latency and implement an explicit cancel/interrupt state in ASP.NET.
- **References:** [Azure Speech service](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/speech-service/) (`speech`); [.NET AI samples](https://github.com/dotnet/ai-samples) (`aisamples`)

### Elective 11: Experiment design and A/B testing
- **Track:** Product analytics
- **Why:** Study statistical uncertainty, confidence intervals, counterfactual thinking, online experiments and user feedback.
- **Try:** Design an A/B trial comparing two RAG retrievers with success/safety guardrails.
- **References:** [Azure Machine Learning documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/machine-learning/) (`azureml`); [Microsoft Foundry documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/) (`foundry`)

### Elective 12: AI governance and secure enterprise platform
- **Track:** Production leadership
- **Why:** Explore compliance, auditability, data retention, tenant isolation, model inventory, safety review and incident response.
- **Try:** Write a model/service inventory, retention policy, threat model and rollback runbook.
- **References:** [OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications](https://genai.owasp.org/llm-top-10/) (`owasp`); [Microsoft Responsible AI resources](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/ai/responsible-ai) (`privacy`)
